## Analysis Summary

### Comparison Snapshot

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Runs covered | `221` |
| Scored events | `1075` |
| Excluded `accidental` events | `11` |
| Overall success rate | `66.7% (717/1075)` |
| `unpudo` success rate | `65.3% (666/1020)` |
| `unparking` success rate | `92.7% (51/55)` |
| Ownership / handover failures | `73.7% (264/358)` |
| Short AV-attempt failures | `32.4% (116/358)` |
| Successful events with no driver accel help | `100.0% (717/717)` |
| Successful events with route change found | `97.4% (698/717)` |
| Successful gear alignment at anchor | `100.0% (717/717)` |

### Failure Profile

- The scored subset contains `358` failures out of `1075` scored events. The dominant effective failure types are `not_av_owned` 50.3% (180/358), `completed_outside_av` 14.5% (52/358), `failed_to_unpudo` 14.2% (51/358).
- Ownership loss and handover breakage are the main theme: `264` of `358` failures fall into `not_av_owned`, `completed_outside_av`, `driver_completed_maneuver`, or `interrupted_handover`.
- Route-change evidence inside failed events is `found` in `273`, `not found` in `2`, and `unclear` in `83` cases.
- Source disengagement labels inside failed events are `failed_to_unpudo` 14.2% (51/358), `failed_to_pudo` 2.8% (10/358), `failed_to_slow` 2.2% (8/358); anything else is failing from DBW / ownership / gear evidence rather than an explicit source disengagement label.
- Event-type split: `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 92.7% (51/55) versus 65.3% (666/1020).

### Success Behavior

- Successful events are distributed as `666` `unpudo` and `51` `unparking`.
- On successful attempts, route change is recovered in `698` of `717` cases and driver accelerator help is absent in `717` of `717` cases.
- Gear state stays aligned at the anchor in `717` of `717` comparable passes. Planned-indicator alignment is `21.9% (157/717)`, and controller-indicator alignment is `21.1% (151/717)`.
- Typical successful timing is median `success baseline -> event start` `14.12s`, median `success baseline -> first motion` `7.92s`, and median AV-owned duration `116.28s`.
- Typical successful maneuver shape: common actual gear at the anchor is `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE`, common actual indicator is `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`, and median max AV speed is `4.78 m/s`.

### Written Assessment

This card covers `1086` recorded events across `221` runs, but only `1075` of them are scored AV-owned attempts. The remaining `11` `accidental` events are explicitly excluded from the success-rate denominator. Within the scored subset, the overall success rate is `66.7% (717/1075)`. `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 92.7% (51/55) versus 65.3% (666/1020).

Across the scored failures, route-change evidence is `273` found / `2` not found / `83` unclear, so navigation context is usually present. The failure mix is led by `not_av_owned` 50.3% (180/358), `completed_outside_av` 14.5% (52/358), `failed_to_unpudo` 14.2% (51/358), while the excluded portion is limited to sub-`2s` accidental AV contact rather than true scored failures.

When this model succeeds, the behavior is consistent: route change is recovered in `698` of `717` successful events, driver accelerator help is absent in `717` of `717`, and gear alignment holds in `717` of `717` comparable passes. The typical successful event starts moving about `7.92s` after the earlier of route change and AV start, reaches a median max AV speed of `4.78 m/s`, and most often begins in gear `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE` with indicator state `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`.
